# Issues

**The register of everything open in this repo.** Defects, gaps, and questions
awaiting a ruling. One id per item, stable forever.

**This file is the source of truth for what is open.** Nothing is open unless it
is here. Other documents no longer carry their own open-item lists — they link
here. Long analysis may stay where it was argued (`packages/*/PROJECT_STATE.md`,
`IDEAS/*.md`); the **Detail** column points at it. What must
never happen again is an item existing in exactly one document that nobody reads.

**What belongs here:** shipped behaviour that is wrong, a gap that blocks
something, a question that needs a decision. **What does not:** features and
roadmap (`IDEAS/`), settled rulings (`DECISIONS.md`), traps that are correct
behaviour you must know about (`CLAUDE.md` § Live hazards), history
(`packages/*/CHANGES.md`).

**Closed items age out to [ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md).**
§ Closed below keeps the recent ones; the archive keeps the rest, verbatim.
Together they hold every id ever issued, and an id is never reused — so a
`FJS-###` named in a comment, a handoff, or a package file resolves in exactly
one of the two files. Search both before concluding an id is unknown.

## Conventions

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Id** | `FJS-###` for a defect or gap, `FJS-D##` for a decision. **Never reused, never renumbered.** Closing an item moves it to § Closed with its id. |
| **Sev** | `S1` blocker — data loss, a security hole, or cannot ship · `S2` high — silently wrong, costs a day to find · `S3` medium · `S4` low or cosmetic |
| **Status** | `open` — verified open on the date shown · `stale?` — inherited from an older audit and **not re-verified**; probe before acting · `contested` — the recorded diagnosis is doubted, see the note |
| **Verified** | The date somebody last ran it. A `stale?` row's date is when it was *written*, not confirmed. |

`stale?` is load-bearing. The eight rows inherited from the 2026-07-31 audit
(now `IDEAS/coherence-review.md`) were re-probed on 2026-08-10 and **five of the eight were wrong**:
two were already fixed (conduit's secrets, `admin:generate`), one was never
reproducible (conduit's version skew), and two were right about a defect and
wrong about what it was — sierra's prefetch is not an auth *bypass* but a
cached 401, and caravan's row named three bugs of which two were fixed.
**Treat a `stale?` row as a lead, not a fact** (`VERIFYING.md`).

**The register also goes stale in the closing direction, which nothing here was
watching for.** `FJS-043` was fixed in the tree with a comment describing the
old defect in the past tense, and stayed `open` for eight days because closing
is a second action nobody owed. A row's `Verified` date is evidence about when
somebody last *looked*, not about whether the defect survives — 22 rows carried
`open` at 2026-08-02 when this audit ran, which is the same fiction `stale?`
exists to name, wearing the other label.

## S1 — blockers

| Id | Pkg | Title | Status | Verified | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="fjs-479"></a>FJS-479 | toolbelt | **`singularize` stripped `es` from every `-ses` plural, so a service named for a `-se` noun had no `@@gate` and no validation** | closed | 2026-08-24 | `singularize('purchases')` answered `purchas`. The `-ses` branch stripped `es` for every word, which is right for `statuses` → `status` and wrong for the rest of the language: `cases` → `cas`, `releases` → `releas`, `licenses` → `licens`, `expenses` → `expens`, `responses` → `respons`, `databases` → `databas`, `warehouses` → `warehous`, `leases` → `leas`, `houses` → `hous`, `phases` → `phas`, `courses` → `cours`. **A round-trip test cannot see it** — `pluralize('cas')` is `cases`, so both directions agree on a word that is not one, and the spec asserted the round trip. `/inflect` is Invariant 2's one owner and junction's `deriveModelName` is a caller: `accessorCandidates('purchases')` answered `['purchases', 'purchas']`, neither matched `purchase`, and `_gateLevels` reads a lookup miss as *no `@@gate` declared*. **Measured against a real Litestone client, not a stand-in: a `find` on a service named `purchases` over `model Purchase { @@gate("4") }` passed `gateAuthAround` with no caller at all, where the identical `posts`/`Post` pair answered 401.** `resolveDefsKey` fails open the same way, so `autoValidate` also does nothing. Not a hypothetical spelling: `Purchase`, `Release`, `License`, `Expense`, `Response`, `Database`, `Warehouse`, `Case`, `Lease`, `Course`, `Phase` are ordinary model names. **Nothing in this repo caught it because no app here has a model ending in `-se`** — the whole class was unreachable from the tree's own schemas. Found by putting a real ERP's 188-model schema through litestone (`packages/litestone/test/fixtures/scale/openmrp.lite`), where a `task_leases` table became `model TaskLeas`. **Fixed 2026-08-24**: strip one `s` by default, strip `es` for an `-ss` stem, and name the closed list of singulars that genuinely end in a bare `s` (`status`, `bus`, `lens`, …) — a list rather than a rule because `status` and `cas` both end in a vowel plus `s` and no ending can separate them. 22 pairs asserted both directions. Green: toolbelt 206, litestone 2986, junction 1403, sierra 1089 | [inflect.js](packages/toolbelt/src/inflect/inflect.js) · [litestone.ts `deriveModelName`](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) · [inflect.spec.js](packages/toolbelt/test/specs/inflect.spec.js) |

| <a id="fjs-525"></a>FJS-525 | caravan | **A daily job at a fixed time runs zero times in spring and twice in autumn, and nothing chose that.** Measured against `America/New_York`: `30 2 * * *` is skipped entirely on the spring boundary (next fire is the following day) and `30 1 * * *` fires **twice** on the autumn one, `05:30Z` and `06:30Z`, both reading *1:30 AM*. **The queue's own idempotency does not cover the second** — a cron fire is dispatched under `cron:<job>:<epoch-minute>` (`FJS-294`) and the two 1:30 AMs are different minutes, so both queue a row and both run. A job that charges a card or sends a customer an email does it twice, once a year, and the alternative failure is silent: the spring job simply does not happen. **The evaluator is not wrong — nothing above it has an opinion**, which is why this is unstated rather than broken: `nextFireTime` answers what the wall clock honestly says, and *what should happen when the wall clock lies* is a question the code never asks. **Untested in either direction**: every `timeZone` assertion in that suite checks that a stated value was stored, and no test anywhere runs a clock across a transition. Ruled `FJS-D144` — adopt Vixie cron's semantics (a fixed-time job fires once per calendar day whatever the clock does; a wildcard schedule follows the new wall clock; a shift over three hours is a clock correction, not DST), and pin all three with tests | open | 2026-08-25 | [cron.ts](packages/caravan/src/cron.ts) · [FJS-D144](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d144) · [FJS-D36](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d36) · [IDEAS/time-and-recurrence.md](IDEAS/time-and-recurrence.md) |
| <a id="fjs-521"></a>FJS-521 | litestone | **A declared `retention 90d` runs once, at `createClient`, and a process that stays up never prunes again.** One call site (`client.js`'s startup loop) and nothing reschedules it, so a server that boots on Monday applies the policy on Monday and not afterwards — the rows the declaration promises to delete accumulate for as long as the deployment is healthy, which is the state a retention policy is written for. `basecamp` declares `database audit { retention 90d }`, so the one instance in the tree is a claim about how long a record is kept. **The cutoff is also a rolling instant rather than a boundary**: `new Date(Date.now() - ms)`, with `d` a flat 86,400,000 and `y` a flat 365 days, so the line sits wherever the process happened to start and no calendar and no zone enter it — *ninety days* means ninety times twenty-four hours measured from a boot. That is the class `IDEAS/time-and-recurrence.md` argues about, already shipping. **And the shipped type is wrong**: `index.d.ts` declares `runSqliteRetention(db, retention)` where the implementation takes `(rawWriteDb, models, retention)`, so a caller reaching for the export writes a call that deletes nothing. The jsonl half has the same shape — compaction runs at `makeJsonlTable` init. What a fix has to settle is ownership rather than code: unattended work with a clock is caravan's (`FJS-D36`) and litestone cannot import it (Invariant 1), so either the client exposes the pass and the app schedules it, or the declaration grows a schedule of its own | open | 2026-08-25 | [tools/retention.js](packages/litestone/src/tools/retention.js) · [client.js:8884](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js#L8884) · [index.d.ts:1172](packages/litestone/src/index.d.ts#L1172) · [IDEAS/time-and-recurrence.md](IDEAS/time-and-recurrence.md) |
| <a id="fjs-519"></a>FJS-519 | litestone | **`asSystem()` is all-or-nothing, and three separate findings are one caller needing to bypass exactly one rule and getting every rule.** It drops the gate, every row policy, `@guarded`, `@system` and every field `@allow` in one move — `if (ctx.isSystem) return null` at [policy.js:442](packages/litestone/src/core/policy.js) and fifteen more sites in `client.js`. That is the right shape for a migration or a seed. It is the wrong shape for the three places in this repo that reach for it, each of which wanted one thing and had to take all of them. **The one that blocks a shipped feature**: declared row tenancy desugars to `@@deny`, which is a policy, so a `@@gate("8")` model can only be read by the client that ignores tenancy — measured on a two-tenant schema, where a scoped caller reads its own row on a reachable model and throws on the gated one, and `asSystem()` reads both tenants on both. A per-tenant credential therefore cannot be enforced declaratively, and doing it in a resolver's own where-clause is exactly what Invariant 6 refuses; `$scopedBy` is not the escape, it binds `@scoped`/`@edge` dimensions and not a tenant ([IDEAS/third-party-credentials.md](IDEAS/third-party-credentials.md) § *The hole*, which states in the record that it is not filed anywhere). **The second is closed and its fix is the evidence**: `FJS-410`'s privilege escalation could not be repaired in the schema — *"no schema rule can reach that path — `members()` is `asSystem()`, because membership is what decides access and cannot be read through the caller it is deciding about"* — so `refuseRoleAboveOwn()` is a hook, and says so out loud at [hooks.ts:230](packages/basecamp/api/src/core/hooks.ts). **The third is why it is filed now**: a capability declared on `WorkspaceMember.setRole` would be enforced by nothing, because all three of its writers are `asSystem()`. Sixteen files under basecamp's `services/` call it. **Not one flag** — the three want different subsets (policies but not the gate · the gate but not tenancy · neither, but still scoped), so the shape is a narrower verb rather than an option bag on this one, and naming the subsets is the design question. Until then the tenancy case is the live one | open | 2026-08-25 | [policy.js](packages/litestone/src/core/policy.js) · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) · [IDEAS/third-party-credentials.md](IDEAS/third-party-credentials.md) · [FJS-410](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md) · [IDEAS/permission-sets.md](IDEAS/permission-sets.md) |
| <a id="fjs-518"></a>FJS-518 | junction · sierra · jetty | **A record read with `get(id)` is a dead object, so every detail screen in the repo goes stale the moment somebody else writes the row.** The live path is the LIST path and only the list path: `client.resource()` opens the socket, wires `created`/`patched`/`removed` into a `Store`, and decides membership and position per event. A detail read goes through the raw proxy — `product = await products.service.get(id)` ([products/\[id\].mesa](example/web/src/routes/products/[id].mesa)), `entry = await portal.service.get(id)` ([ServiceHealthBody.mesa](packages/basecamp/web/src/components/widgets/ServiceHealthBody.mesa)) — and lands in a plain variable no announcement can reach. The framework's headline guarantee is that you never invalidate, because the server says what changed; on a detail screen that guarantee is simply false, and it is false in the silent-wrong-data class — the page renders, the numbers are stale, nothing throws. **Two rows of one model can also disagree with each other**: the store is per-`resource()` call (`const store = new Store<T>()` inside `resource()`), so two `createResource('orders')` in two route files are two copies, kept in step only because both subscribe to the same service. Nothing keys a row to an identity, which is also why nothing can mark a value provisional or put one back — the reason no optimistic update exists anywhere in the repo. **Ruled 2026-08-25 rather than patched**: a node per row keyed by model, a list as a view over it, a detail as a view of one, a TTL for lifetime, and an overlay carrying intent — [FJS-D138](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d138), which carries the build order. jetty's copy of the store is the same defect one package over ([FJS-493](#fjs-493)) and is fixed by importing rather than by a second repair. **Fixed 2026-08-25**, as `FJS-D138` sequenced it. `packages/junction/src/client/nodes.ts` is the registry — one node per row, keyed by MODEL and by a normalised id, because a list holds `{ id: 5 }` and a detail screen is reached by a URL carrying `'5'`, and two nodes there means the push moves exactly one of them. `Store` is a VIEW now: bound it holds ids and materialises through the registry, so `store.get()` still answers rows and **no screen changed** — `useStore` is the one bridge to a Mesa signal and nothing in either app calls `subscribe` or `get` directly. Unbound it is what it always was, which is what the fifteen `Store` cases in `client.test.ts` are the negative control for. Lifetime is a **TTL** (`nodeTtlMs`, default 30s) rather than a reference count the application maintains, which is the part of Apollo and Relay everyone complains about. `resource.record(id)` is the view of one; Sierra's wraps it so the first read goes through the resource's own `_call('get')` — its hooks, its coercion, its `@version` — while Junction keeps the rule about WHEN to read. **A push moves the value and does not move the remembered version**, which is `FJS-341` restated as a test: the node is the synced truth and the view is what this screen READ. `example`'s order detail is the first caller and its two manual `await load()` after an action are gone — that reload was the app hiding this. **Proven in a browser**: `verify:live` gained a third question (17 assertions, up from 14) — a watcher tab on the detail page, the order paid from node over HTTP on a session of its own, the status moving with `sameDocument: true`. Negative-controlled: reverted to `service.get(id)` it reads `{"status":"pending","moved":false}`. Two things fell out on the way: `remove` drops `$withDeleted`, so the drive could not clean up after itself and had only ever passed on a fresh seed ([FJS-523](#fjs-523)); and jetty's copy is untouched by design — it gets this by importing rather than by a second repair ([FJS-493](#fjs-493)). Green: junction 1437 (+20), sierra 1124 (+7), typecheck clean both | closed | 2026-08-25 | [client/index.ts](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts) · [resource.js](packages/sierra/src/junction/resource.js) · [FJS-D138](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d138) · [IDEAS/client-data-lifecycle.md](IDEAS/client-data-lifecycle.md) |
| <a id="fjs-523"></a>FJS-523 | junction | **A soft-deleted row's `@unique` value cannot be released through a service, because `remove` never reads `$withDeleted`.** Litestone's escape hatch is `delete({ …, withDeleted: true })` — *stop keeping the row* — and it is the documented way out of `SoftDeletedUniqueError`, which is a 409 telling a caller their value is held by a row they cannot see. The directive crosses the bridge: `$withDeleted` is in `@frontierjs/toolbelt/directives`, `parseQuery` reads it, and `find` and `get` both pass it through (`if (q.withDeleted) args.withDeleted = true`, four call sites). **`remove` and `patch` do not.** So `DELETE /api/orders/6?$withDeleted=true` on a soft-deleted row answers `404 orders with id=6 not found` — the existence probe runs under `softDeleteFilter()` and the directive is parsed and dropped. **Measured** against `example` on 2026-08-25: the row is plainly there (`GET /api/orders?reference=…&$withDeleted=true` answers it, `deletedAt` set), and there is no request an app can make that frees the reference. Found by `verify:live`, which had been passing only on a freshly seeded database: it deletes its order at the end, the delete is soft, `Order.reference` is `@unique`, and the next run's create is a 409 naming a row no ordinary query can see — which reads as a broken app rather than as a drive that cannot clean up after itself. Worked around there by minting a reference per run, the same answer `verify:money` gives for its discount codes; the workaround is not the fix. **Two things to decide rather than assume.** Whether `remove` honouring it is enough or `patch` needs it too — the hazard note says `update({ …, withDeleted: true })` moves a deleted row's value, which is the other half of the same escape. And whether a hard delete should be reachable from a browser at all, since it is the one write that defeats `@@softDelete`; if not, the honest answer is a refusal naming the flag rather than a 404 naming the row. **The `update`/`patch` half shipped 2026-08-26 and it is the one that closes the hole**: moving the value aside is litestone's own step 1 and needs no new verb — `PATCH /api/orders/{id}?$withDeleted=true` reaches the row, `writeWhere(q)` is the one place the filter is lifted, and the default stays fail-closed (the same request without the directive is still a 404). It lifts on BOTH paths — litestone's own filter and junction's `softDelete:` override — or the directive would work on one kind of service and not the other. A **bulk** patch deliberately does not: `bulkByRow` counts and selects through `table.count`/`findMany`, which apply litestone's filter, so widening the WHERE alone would match nothing and read as the directive being ignored. **`remove` stays open and is the whole of what is left**, because against an already-deleted row the only remaining action is to stop keeping it — a hard delete — and giving `DELETE ?$withDeleted=true` that meaning is a semantic decision rather than a passthrough; it is asserted as still refused, so making it work has to be deliberate. 7 tests against a real Litestone client. **`example`'s `verify` is what proved the hole was live**: `Order` gained `@@softDelete` and the drive's stale-reference sweep stopped working, so it passed once per seed and then reported *the create page is broken* — it releases the value now instead of deleting an already-deleted row | open | 2026-08-26 | [litestone.ts `remove`](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) · [directives.js](packages/toolbelt/src/directives/directives.js) · [verify-live.mjs](example/web/test/verify-live.mjs) |
| <a id="fjs-517"></a>FJS-517 | basecamp | **Three of `Deployment`'s seven declared moves are driven by nothing, and three screens colour the states they lead to.** `@@transitions(status, …)` declares `push: building -> pushing`, `release: pushing -> deploying` and `rollback: success -> rolled_back @gate(5)`. Nothing under `api/` names any of those moves or any of those states: the pipeline is `pending -> building -> success` or `failed`, written as ordinary column updates in `deployments.service.ts` and the deploy job. So `pushing` and `deploying` are unreachable, and `rollback` is an authority level (`@gate(5)`, deliberately above the model's update gate — the schema explains why at the declaration) for a move no code path makes. **The UI already believes in them**: `DeployFeedBody.mesa`, `routes/deployments/index.mesa` and `routes/apps/[id]/index.mesa` each map a tone for `pushing`, `deploying` and `rolled_back`, so three screens carry a colour for a state that cannot occur. Not a correctness fault — litestone enforces the machine on every write, so nothing illegal happens; it is a declaration nobody drives, which reads exactly like a feature somebody has not got round to. **Two honest answers and the choice is a product one**: drive the pipeline through push and release (they describe real stages an image build has) and implement rollback, or delete the three clauses and the three tone entries. | <a id="fjs-481"></a>FJS-481 | jetty · mesa | **A mesa rename moved the shape jetty's build plugin matches by TEXT, so every component with a delegated event stopped hot-updating and ~450 green tests said nothing** | closed | 2026-08-24 | `injectJettyHMR` wraps compiled Mesa so a component registers itself with `globalThis.__jettyMesa` and can be swapped in place. It does that with two regexes over the compiler's OUTPUT — a shape jetty reads and does not own — and the second one's lookahead was `(?=\n\$runtime\.\$\$delegate|\s*$)`. **`FJS-470` renamed every emitted identifier to `$$`**, so from that day the lookahead matched nothing for any component carrying a delegated event, which is nearly all of them: Step 1 still renamed the function, Step 2 injected no registration, and the module exported a wrapper that mounts correctly and never registers. The symptom is an edit that does not appear — no error, no warning, a green build. Measured against real compiler output: `Step 1 matches: true · Step 2 matches: false`. **Nothing caught it, and the reason is its own finding.** The only cover was `test/hmr-fullflow.mjs` and `test/hmr-integration.mjs`, which no script runs — and could not have run: they import mesa by an **absolute path from another machine** (`/home/claude/repo/reading-list/frontierjs/mesa/compiler.js`), so they fail at module resolution. jetty's ten phase files, ~450 assertions, had never compiled a component. `fli check`'s `test-files-run` could not see them either, which is [FJS-482](#fjs-482). **Fixed 2026-08-24, three ways.** The lookahead matches the delegate call by SHAPE (`\$+runtime`) rather than by the namespace's name. **The plugin now throws when neither step lands**, naming the file and saying the shape has moved — a wrapper that mounts and never registers is worse than an unwrapped module, and this is a build-time answer instead of one at somebody's desk. And `test/phase9.test.js` drives `injectJettyHMR` with **real compiler output**, both branches of the lookahead plus the unrecognised-shape throw; negative-controlled, the old lookahead turns 3 of its 7 red. The two dead harnesses moved to `packages/jetty/docs/dead/` with a README saying why they are kept — the full-flow shape through jsdom is worth repairing one day and `phase9` covers only the wrapper's output. **`FJS-D134`'s class, one layer out**: the ruling is about compiled output meeting compiled output, and this is compiled output meeting a BUILD PLUGIN that matches it with a regex, where nothing at either end declares the dependency | [mesa-plugin.js:259](packages/jetty/src/build/mesa-plugin.js#L259) · [phase9.test.js](packages/jetty/test/phase9.test.js) · [FJS-470](#fjs-470) |
| <a id="fjs-482"></a>FJS-482 | mesa · cli | **A component could declare the compiler's own calling convention — `function __anchor(){}` threw at mount and `const __prev = 9` rendered `[object Object]`** | closed | 2026-08-24 | `__anchor` and `__props` are the component function's parameters, `__block` is threaded into every `track()` call, and `__prev` is the render callback's. None was in `BUILTIN_LOCALS`, so an author could declare any of them at the top level of a script — where the declaration lands in the SAME scope and wins. **Unlike `FJS-471`'s builtins this is a shadow rather than a redeclaration**, so the output is valid JavaScript and the failure moves to runtime or nowhere: `function __anchor(){}` compiles, parses, and throws at mount with `anchor.before is not a function`; `const __prev = 9` compiles and renders **`[object Object]`**, silently, because the callback's parameter shadows the author's constant. `function __block()`, `function __props()` and `class __dev {}` survived the cases probed — `__dev` is `$$runtime.__dev`, a property rather than a scope name, and cannot be shadowed at all. **Fixed by reserving the four**, which refuses the declaration rather than renaming anything: the names are protocol (jetty's build plugin matches `__anchor, __props, __block` as text — [FJS-481](#fjs-481)), so moving them is `FJS-D134`'s decision and not this one. The message is split by kind, because the existing one says *the compiler injects it into the same scope, and two declarations is a SyntaxError* and that is the wrong sentence here — nothing is injected and there is no SyntaxError. A nested scope is deliberately untouched: a parameter of the author's own function shadows nothing of the compiler's. 5 cases in `emission.test.js`. **The open remainder is the naming decision itself** — `FJS-470` closed saying `__anchor`/`__block`/`__props` did not converge on `$$` and that half *stays open below*, and there was no row below: it was tracked nowhere until this one. The population is three kinds, which is what the convergence framing missed — the calling convention (four names, author-reachable, this issue), runtime PROPERTIES (`$$runtime.__dev`, `n.__resolving`, unshadowable), and generated locals inside emitted callbacks. Only the first group can collide, three of its four are protocol, and with them reserved the four-line rule is broken in appearance rather than in consequence | [compiler.js `BUILTIN_LOCALS`](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) · [FJS-470](#fjs-470) · [FJS-D134](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d134) |
| <a id="fjs-478"></a>FJS-478 | mesa | **`bind:class` on an element compiled to a write on a DOM property that does not exist, so neither direction ever touched the element** | closed | 2026-08-24 | `VISION` § 10.8 documented class passthrough as *two shorthand forms* — `{class}` one-way and `bind:class` two-way, *for components that mutate their own class set and want the parent to observe the change*. The second one has never worked. `bind:class` and its `:class` alias parse into `bind:class={$class}` and then take the **form-value path**, whose generic branch is `const handler = () => set(el[name])` plus `createEffect(() => { el[name] = readFn() ?? '' })`. With `name === 'class'` that is `el['class']`, and **there is no such DOM property** — it is `className`. Measured against a real DOM: after `el.class = 'raised'`, `getAttribute('class')` and `className` both still read `own hash` and only `el.class` reads back `raised`, which is a JS expando nothing renders. So the write reached nothing and the read-back read its own write. **Clean compile, valid JavaScript, a binding that silently does not exist.** **Nothing caught it because nothing used it** — zero `.mesa` files in this repo, across `packages/ui`'s 69 components and every app — and `compiler.test.js` had a case that PINNED it: *bind:class in child auto-wires $class*, asserting `bindInput` was emitted, which is precisely the broken output. The test passed for as long as the feature was broken. **Refused rather than fixed, 2026-08-24.** The refusal names `{class}` and says why. Fixing it would be a different feature: the observe direction needs a `MutationObserver` on `attributes`, which is a mechanism nobody wrote, for a use case with no user. **No comparable framework offers a two-way class binding** — Svelte has `class:name` and the clsx-style `class`, Vue merges class through fallthrough attributes automatically and reserves `v-model` for form input, Angular has `[class.x]` and `[(ngModel)]` — and where a framework DOES observe element state it makes the binding readonly, which is what Svelte's `bind:clientWidth` is. If the observe direction is ever wanted it is a readonly binding with a new name, not a repair of this one. **On a COMPONENT `bind:class` is untouched** and is an ordinary two-way prop through `bindProp`; the refusal is on the element path alone, which is the only route to `bindInput(el, 'class', …)`. `RULE 31b` in VISION, 4 cases in `emission.test.js` covering the accepted surface and the three refused spellings, and the pinning test replaced with the refusal. Green: mesa 1285 + 82 + 47, sierra 1089, ui 857 | [compiler.js:5703](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js#L5703) · [runtime.js](packages/mesa/src/runtime.js) `bindInput` · [VISION.md § 10.8](packages/mesa/docs/VISION.md) |
| <a id="fjs-477"></a>FJS-477 | mesa | **A compiled door member emitted a reference to a local nothing declared — `$.mounted` worked in one spelling out of four and `$.context.use` in none** | closed | 2026-08-24 | `$.mounted`, `$.context` and `$.inspect` are the three door members that are COMPILED rather than read: a pre-parse AST pass rewrites `$.x` to `$x`, and the local that identifier resolves to is emitted only where a sniff found the shape the compiler knows how to wire. The sniff for `$mounted` was a **regex over the script text** demanding `const NAME = $mounted(`, so three other spellings rewrote to `$mounted` and emitted a reference to a binding that was never declared — `$.mounted(fn)` as a bare call, `const m = $.mounted`, and `{$.mounted}` in a template. **Clean compile, valid JavaScript, `ReferenceError` on first render**, which is Invariant 15's failure in its exact shape. `$.context` had the same hole in a wider form: the compiler consumes the SUGAR (`const v = $.context.k` becomes a `$$ctxRead` + `trackDerived`, `$.context.k = v` becomes a `$$ctxProvide`) and passes the runtime object through untouched — so `$.context.use('k')` and `$.context.provide('k', fn)`, which are a real export (`runtime.js`'s `context = { use, provide }`), emitted `$context.use(…)` against an undeclared `$context`. **Every documented imperative use of context was broken and nothing in the repo used it.** A template made it worse rather than the same: the rewrite is script-only, so `{$.context.k}` reached output verbatim and read `.k` off `{ use, provide }` — `undefined` where the door member existed, a `TypeError` where it did not. **Not a regression from `FJS-D132`** — `$mounted(fn)` as a bare call failed identically before the door — but the bare spelling is refused now, so `$.` is the only way in and it invites three spellings that do not work. **Fixed 2026-08-24.** The gate reads the AST the rewrite produced instead of the text: `refuseCompiledDoorMisuse` blesses the shapes the compiler wires and refuses every surviving `$mounted` by name with the working spelling stated, and answers whether a `$context` reference survived the sugar — which is what now requires the local, so both imperative forms work anywhere. `refuseCompiledDoorInTemplate` refuses `$.mounted` and the `$.context.<key>` sugar in a template, naming the script form; `.use`/`.provide` pass. **The first cut of the template rule was a text scan and it refused a REPL example** whose markup contains the sentence *It provides $.context.count* — prose about the framework is what an example page is made of. Both template scans now read `templateExpressions()`, every `{ … }` region by brace depth, which also closed the same latent trap in `refuseBareBuiltins`. **Also removed: `findContextInBody`**, a dead validator in pass 5 carrying a live `ReferenceError` on an out-of-scope `n` — unreachable, so it had never fired. 12 cases in `emission.test.js`, negative-controlled (7 red with the fix neutered). Green: mesa 1277 + 82 + 47, sierra 1088, ui 857 | [compiler.js:2099](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js#L2099) · [compiler.js:2019](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js#L2019) · [FJS-D132](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d132) |
| <a id="fjs-458"></a>FJS-458 | junction | **Service autoload defaults to `services/` beside the ENTRY, which is not where the layout the framework documents and scaffolds puts them — and the miss is silent** | open | 2026-08-23 | `autoload-services` resolves `dirname(Bun.main)/services` when neither `opts.autoload` nor `_junction.services.dir` is set. That is the FLAT layout, where the entry and the services are siblings. The canonical layout (root README § Project Structure, and what `fli new` writes) puts the entry at `api/index.ts` and the services at `api/src/services/`, so the default resolves to `api/services` — a directory that is not there. **Nothing fails**: `autoloadServices` is not called, the app boots, `/health` answers, and every route those services would have mounted is a 404. **Measured** on `example` while moving it to the canonical layout: the boot line went from `services=12` to `services=3` (the three auth registers, which are not autoloaded), the app came up clean, and the drive's only symptom was `api never answered on /api/products` — a URL that is a 404 rather than a refusal. `fli new` does not hit this because `makeJunctionConfig` declares `services.dir` explicitly, which means the default is unreachable for a scaffolded app and load-bearing for every hand-written or regenerated one. Three candidate fixes: fall back to `dirname(Bun.main)/src/services` when the sibling directory is absent; resolve against the app root rather than the entry; or keep the default and WARN by name when the resolved directory does not exist, which is the cheap half and the one that makes the other two safe to argue about. A directory that is simply absent is currently indistinguishable from an app that declares no services on purpose, and the app's own `surface.snapshot.md` cannot see it either — `junction surface` is passed `--services` by hand | [app.ts](packages/junction/src/core/app.ts) `autoload-services` · [new.md](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md) `makeJunctionConfig` · [FJS-449](#fjs-449) |
| <a id="fjs-449"></a>FJS-449 | litestone | **A relative `database { path }` resolves against the process CWD, so a command run from the wrong directory mints a second, empty database and every tool then reports on that one** | open | 2026-08-23 | `resolveDbPath` ends in node's one-argument `resolve(v)`, which resolves against `process.cwd()`; the schema file's own directory is never in scope, and `openSqliteConnections` pre-creates a missing parent, so there is nothing left to fail. **Measured live in `example`**: a `litestone studio --schema schema.lite --port 8502` started from `example/db/` had been serving `example/db/db/shop.db` — 4,096 bytes, one empty header page — for 19 hours, while the real database sat two directories up at 196,608 bytes. Studio showed empty tables and nothing anywhere said why. Two more of the same shape were beside it: `example/web/db/shop.db`, from a command run in the web root, which is where sierra's own commands are documented to run; and `example/development.db`, from litestone's no-config default (`cli.js` `./development.db`). **All three were invisible**, which is what makes this S2 rather than an annoyance — the root `.gitignore`'s `*.db*` matches at any depth, so `git status` is clean and the only way to find them is to go looking. The schema carries a comment warning about exactly this for its `audit` block and nothing acts on it. Three candidate fixes and they are not the same change: resolve against the schema file's directory, which is a behaviour change and is probably right (a path written beside the seed means beside the seed); refuse to open a database whose resolved path is outside the app root; or warn, which is the cheap half — `fli`'s `core/db-preflight.js` already parses that path for its own reasons, though it cannot cover litestone's own CLI, which is what produced all three of these. Junk cleaned 2026-08-23; the studio's copy survives while its process holds it open. **Measured again 2026-08-23 in a new tool and with a worse consequence**: `vite build` on `example/site` runs from the SURFACE root, so `./db/shop.db` resolved to `example/site/db/shop.db` — a fresh empty database, minted by the build — and the storefront prerendered **12 product pages as 0 products, exit 0**. A published static site with no products in it, and nothing anywhere said why; the only reason it was caught is that the drive counts emitted pages against a query. A prerendered artefact is the sharpest version of this, because it is public the moment it ships and cannot be recalled. Worked around in the app (`SHOP_DB_PATH=../db/shop.db` on `build:site` and `dev:site`), which is a per-app patch and not the fix | [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) `resolveDbPath` · [cli.js](packages/litestone/src/tools/cli.js) · [.gitignore](.gitignore) **Partly fixed 2026-08-23, and the fix is narrower than this row assumed.** Both remedies proposed above are unavailable: paths are written against the APP ROOT, not the schema — `db/schema.lite` says `./db/shop.db`, naming a sibling from one level up — so anchoring to the schema's own directory produces exactly the junk path; and the apps that hit this assemble their schema in MEMORY, so there is no location to anchor to at all. What shipped is `createClient({ resolveFrom: 'schema' })`, anchoring to the app root (the schema's directory, or its parent when that directory is `db`), **opt-in** with the litestone CLI opting in at all eight of its client call sites and in its own second copy of the resolver, which now imports `schemaAnchor` rather than restating it. **Opt-in because unconditional broke basecamp**: its seed test isolates a run with a scratch `cwd`, redirecting `database main` by env var and `database audit` — which has no env var — by the CWD alone, so anchoring sent that audit log back to the shared `db/audit/` and the suite failed on a locked database. CWD resolution is an isolation contract, not an accident, and basecamp's `core/db.ts` documents relying on it. Two observability halves went with it and mattered as much: `describeDataRealm` printed every path RELATIVE TO THE CWD, so the catastrophic run and the correct one logged the identical string `./db/shop.db` — the cwd is printed beside them now; and `litestone studio`'s `db:` line tested `cfg.db`, which always defaults to `./development.db`, so the branch naming the real declared databases had never once executed. Reproduced against the fix: studio from `example/db/` creates no `db/db/` and names the real file. 7 tests, one pinning the default-is-CWD guarantee. **Still open for the embedded half** — an app assembling a schema string, which is what prerendered `example/site` was doing |

| <a id="fjs-528"></a>FJS-528 | litestone | **The two spellings of declared tenancy give OPPOSITE answers about a row that belongs to no tenant, and both are deliberate.** A nullable claim COLUMN desugars to `workspaceId != auth().workspaceId`, and a null row is invisible to every tenant — the documented intent, ruled in [FJS-D141](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d141) and measured (`tenant A sees: [ "a-row" ]`, the null row absent, `asSystem()` sees all three). A DELEGATED model over an OPTIONAL relation desugars to `check(rel)`, which [FJS-382](#fjs-382) deliberately compiled as `FK IS NULL OR EXISTS (…)` — so a child with no parent is visible to **every** tenant (`tenant A sees: [ "a-note", "orphan-note" ]`). Both readings defend themselves — a column is the row's own claim, where a null relation is a row nobody has filed yet — and `verifyTenantIsolation` already reports the delegated case as `unparented` rather than grading it, which is the honest position while it is unsettled. **Not a live defect and filed rather than fixed**: reversing either half is a behaviour change with tests pinning the current answer, and nothing is waiting on it. What is owed is a ruling that makes the two agree or says why they should not, because the next person to write `Parent?` on a scoped model will reason from whichever half they read first | open | 2026-08-25 | [multi-tenancy.md](packages/litestone/docs/multi-tenancy.md) · [core/policy.js § check](packages/litestone/src/core/policy.js) · [FJS-382](#fjs-382) |
| <a id="fjs-526"></a>FJS-526 | sierra · ui | **An edit form sends the server's own columns straight back, so `<Form record={row}>` is a 403 about a column that is not on the screen.** `@system`, `@generated`, `@computed`, `@from`, `@version` and a tenancy stamp all reach the browser as `readOnly`, and two things already read that flag: a generated form does not offer the control, and `make()` does not seed the value. Neither covers an EDIT form, which is handed a row the SERVER wrote — carrying every column the caller could READ — and writes the whole record back. The Data boundary then refuses `@system` **by name**, deliberately, because a payload naming one is code that meant to write it ([FJS-D28](DECISIONS.md)). So the refusal is correct at every layer and the person is shown *`Customer.userId` is @system…* under a form whose every visible control they filled in legitimately. **Found on `example`'s customers screen the first time it had an edit form at all**: `Customer.userId` is `@system`, and every save failed. Not reachable from the create path, which is why nothing here had seen it — a create starts from `make()`, and `make()` skips exactly these columns. **Fixed 2026-08-26**: `stripReadOnly(fields, data, { keep })` in sierra's `field-rules.js` — the leaf module, so it runs in plain node — called first in `_call`'s create/patch pipeline, ahead of coerce/blank/validate, so nothing downstream judges a value that is not going to be sent. It takes a **keep list rather than dropping every read-only key**, because the `@version` column is `readOnly` and is the one the server REQUIRES back: that is not an exception to the rule so much as the reason the rule cannot be spelled *delete every readOnly key*. A key with no rule behind it is left alone — a `@transient` or a custom method's own argument is legitimate, and guessing about those is how a strip becomes the thing that breaks a working app. 13 tests, three of them through `createResource().save()` rather than the pure function, because a refactor dropping the call would leave the unit tests green and put the 403 straight back. Green: sierra 1137, basecamp `verify` 302/302 and `verify:screens` 26/26 | closed | 2026-08-26 | [field-rules.js](packages/sierra/src/junction/field-rules.js) · [resource.js](packages/sierra/src/junction/resource.js) · [resource-readonly.test.js](packages/sierra/tests/resource-readonly.test.js) · [customers/index.mesa](example/web/src/routes/customers/index.mesa) |
| <a id="fjs-527"></a>FJS-527 | example | **`bun run reset` reseeded the shop and left the job queue, so a dead row held its `unique` key against every later run.** The queue is a separate SQLite file (`db/jobs.db`) and the reset deleted only `db/shops`, `db/shops-registry.db` and `db/audit`. A `book-courier` row enqueued by an earlier run therefore survived the reseed that deleted the staff member it recorded, and retried forever with `app.runAs(…) — no such principal`. Caravan's `unique` is a lock on work that is still owed and a `pending` row still holds it, so `book-courier:5` was held against every future `ship` of order 5 and **the dispatch was a silent no-op**. **What it reads as is the misdirection worth filing**: `verify:jobs` fails `job.wroteTracking` while `job.record` PASSES, because the row the drive finds is the previous run's, done and correct — so the evidence says *the job ran and did not write*, which is a framework bug that is not there. `verify:notify`'s `announce-payment` went the same way, and a full sweep showed the two as flakes because each was green the first time after a manual delete. Caravan is right and the reset was wrong: work that is still owed should hold its key across a backoff. **Fixed 2026-08-26** — `reset` deletes `db/jobs.db*` too, and `PROJECT_STATE.md` § Working notes says why, since the symptom points at the wrong package | closed | 2026-08-26 | [example/package.json](example/package.json) · [PROJECT_STATE.md](example/PROJECT_STATE.md) · [verify-jobs.mjs](example/web/test/verify-jobs.mjs) |

Passing the schema OBJECT is worse still: the seed stops being the runtime owner and a stale artefact validates against last week's rules. **The rule that decides it, and it explains the three existing choices rather than contradicting them**: an early-bound handle where the thing pointed at is hand-written in the same language (caravan's `defineJob`), a name plus a registry where it is generated from another one (`input:`) or where the boundary cannot import (`registerControl`). | <a id="fjs-480"></a>FJS-480 | litestone | **`@@index([deletedAt])` beside `@@softDelete` emitted the same index name twice, so the schema parsed and the database could not be created** | closed | 2026-08-24 | `@@softDelete` emits its own partial index as `idx_<table>_deletedAt`, and a declared `@@index([deletedAt])` derives the identical name. The DDL is emitted, the run reaches SQLite, and the boot dies on `index idx_note_deletedAt already exists` — an error naming a physical index, about a schema that passed validation with no warning. Eight lines reproduce it: `model Note { id Int @id  body String  deletedAt DateTime?  @@index([deletedAt])  @@softDelete }`. Declaring an index on the soft-delete column is the obvious thing to do for anyone who has not read that the attribute already does it, and it is what an introspected schema produces — found converting a real MySQL schema, where the source table had exactly that index. Two ways out and they differ: **refuse at parse**, naming the attribute that already provides it, or **dedupe at emit** and say nothing. **Refused at parse, 2026-08-24.** The declaration buys nothing either way — a declared index on a soft-delete table is already given the `WHERE "deletedAt" IS NULL` clause, so it compiled to exactly the index `@@softDelete` was about to write, and what a dedupe would preserve is the ability to write a line with no effect. Single-column only: `@@index([deletedAt, status])` derives a different name and stays an ordinary composite. The refusal names both attributes and the way out. 4 cases in `test/soft-delete-index.test.ts`, negative-controlled (2 red with the check neutered). `litestone introspect` does not produce the pair — checked rather than assumed: it emits model-level `@@index` for composites only and drops solo non-unique indexes altogether, which is a separate gap. So the shape arrives from a person writing the obvious thing, or from a converter reading another database's DDL, which is where this one came from. Green: litestone 2993 | [parser.js](packages/litestone/src/core/parser.js) · [ddl.js `createIndexes`](packages/litestone/src/core/ddl.js) · [soft-delete-index.test.ts](packages/litestone/test/soft-delete-index.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-471"></a>FJS-471 | mesa | **Nine compiler-injected builtin names emit invalid JavaScript when an author declares them with `const`, `var` or `function`, and the compile reports no error** | closed | 2026-08-23 | The builtins are injected into the component init function as a run of `const` declarations — `const $option = { props: __props }`, `const $slots = $runtime.makeSlots(__block)`, `const $onMount = $runtime.$onMount` and their neighbours. An author who declares the same name at script top level gets a **second** declaration in the same scope, which is a duplicate-binding `SyntaxError`. **Measured** across four declaration forms × thirteen names, compiling and then parsing the output with acorn as `sourceType: 'module'`: `let` is safe on every name, because a reactive `let` is renamed to `$$sig_<name>` before emit; `const`, `var` and `function` each produce invalid output on nine — `$option`, `$slots`, `$onMount`, `$onDestroy`, `$onCleanup`, `$props`, `$attributes`, `$context`, `$emit` — for **27 silently-invalid combinations**. Negative control: `$zzz` and a plain identifier compile and parse in all four forms, and the compiler INTERNALS (`$runtime`, `$dom`, `$class`, `$parentElement`) are clean in all four, so the surface is exactly the injected builtin set and not the `$`-prefixed internals. **The compile reports `errors: none` in all 27**, which is Invariant 15 exactly — a clean compile is not proof of valid JS — so the failure surfaces as a parse error in generated code in the browser, pointing at a line the author did not write. `const $context = …` is the plausible trigger: an author aliasing the context object locally is a natural thing to reach for, and it is one of the nine. **`FJS-D132` retires this class by construction** rather than by patching it — one injected name instead of twelve, and a shadow of `$` is one of the three refusals that ruling already requires — but the fix is worth landing FIRST and on its own, because it is small, it is the only part of that work with a user-visible defect behind it, it builds the refusal machinery the ruling needs later, and it is valuable whether or not the namespace change proceeds. The test must PARSE the emitted output, not merely assert a clean compile; asserting the compile is what let this run. Filed S3 rather than S2 because the trigger requires the author to pick one of nine specific names; the cost once triggered is S2-shaped — silent at compile, loud and misdirected at runtime | **Fixed 2026-08-23.** `BUILTIN_LOCALS` names the thirteen injected names beside `RESERVED_WORDS`, and `analyzeScript` walks its own `ast.body` — not `vars`, which carries neither function nor class declarations, both of which collided — refusing every declaration form by name. It THROWS rather than pushing to `errors`, because the call site downgrades that whole array to warnings and a module that does not parse is not a warning. Scoped to the factory, so a nested `const $props` is still legal; over-refusing there would break code that never collided. Verified 65/65 refusals across five declaration forms including destructuring, with eight controls still compiling and parsing. Seven cases added to `test/emission.test.js`. Green: mesa 1233 vitest + 82 runtime-browser + 47 vite-browser, `@frontierjs/ui` 857 with 69/69 components opened. The surface was found to be 13 names rather than the 9 first measured — `$inspect` collides only when `debug !== false`, and `$ctxProvide`/`$ctxRead` only where `$context` is in use, which is why the reserved set is keyed off what the compiler MAY inject rather than what a given config does inject | [compiler.js](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) · [emission.test.js](packages/mesa/test/emission.test.js) · [FJS-D132](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d132) · [IDEAS/mesa-dollar-namespace.md](IDEAS/mesa-dollar-namespace.md) |
| <a id="fjs-483"></a>FJS-483 | cli · litestone · auth · basecamp | **Nothing detected an app that pastes a package's models instead of importing them, and a paste drifts in silence** | closed | 2026-08-24 | `extend model` (2026-08-24) removed the REASON to paste: an app can import `@frontierjs/auth/schema.lite` and still add the relation back to its own `User`, `@@log(audit)` and `@@tenant(none)`. What it did not do is stop the paste, and the cost of one is measured rather than argued — basecamp carried hand copies of four auth models for its whole life, and one of them had `@guarded(all)` where the package writes `@secret`, so turning OAuth on there would have stored every provider access and refresh token unencrypted. **137 tests were green either side of the divergence**, because nothing anywhere compares a copy to its original. **Filed with the wrong rule in mind, and that was the whole difficulty.** *An app declares `model X` where a dependency also declares `model X`* fires on every correct install: `@frontierjs/auth` ships one file to be IMPORTED and one — `User` — to be APPENDED and grown, so the copy itself decides nothing, and the filed note concluded a package needed a new way to say which was which. It does not. **What is decidable is a column THE PACKAGE DECLARES, declared differently here.** Adding a column is what an app is for; adding `@@tenant(none)` or a policy is the app's business too; changing the package's own column is the class that costs something. No manifest key, no language change, no parser — a line scan over a file in `node_modules`, reached through that package's own `exports` map. **Fixed 2026-08-24** as `fli check`'s twenty-seventh rule, `package-model-drift`. A WARNING and not an error, because a deviation can be right and the reader has to judge: both declarations are printed, and `check-baseline.json` is how an app accepts one it has argued for. **Its first run on basecamp found three things and closed all three, one of them in the package rather than the app.** (1) `User.emailVerified` and (2) `User.role` had lost `@allow('write', auth().isAdmin)` — basecamp gates update at USER(4) with `@@allow('update', id == auth().id)`, *your own row*, so the field policy is the only thing between a caller and their own `emailVerified`, and marking your own address verified is skipping the verification. **Latent rather than live**: basecamp exposes no `users` service and its gate grades on `status` and `WorkspaceMember.role`, neither of which is this — it becomes real the day a profile screen writes through a service over `db.user`. Both restored, and both now appear in `db/access.snapshot.md`, so a future removal is a diff. (3) `User.accountId` was `String?` against auth's `Int?` — and here the PACKAGE was wrong: every id `@frontierjs/auth` declares is a uuid, so an `Int?` could not hold one, which is what made the column unusable for the one app that reached for it. auth ships `String?` now; `SessionContext.accountId` was always a string either way. Seven cases in `tests/checks.test.js`, mutation-checked both directions — never comparing, and comparing the APP's columns instead of the package's (the false-positive shape), each turn it red. Green: cli 848, auth 244, basecamp 143 + 301/301 | [checks.js](packages/cli/core/checks.js) · [checks.test.js](packages/cli/tests/checks.test.js) · [user.lite](packages/auth/db/user.lite) · [schema.lite](packages/basecamp/db/schema.lite) |
| <a id="fjs-456"></a>FJS-456 | sierra | **A prerendered site's `sitemap.xml` lists no dynamic page, so a storefront's sitemap omits every product** | open | 2026-08-23 | `generateSitemap` is handed `routeTable.indexed`, which is filtered to exclude dynamic routes — correct for an SPA, where `/products/:slug/` is not a URL — and the static build then emits one file per `getStaticPaths()` entry and tells the sitemap nothing about them. **Measured** on `example/site`: 15 pages emitted, `sitemap.xml (3 URLs)` — `/`, `/404/`, `/catalog/` — so the twelve pages the site exists to publish are the twelve it does not list. (`/404/` being IN it is a second, smaller wrong: a not-found page is the one page that should never be indexed.) The build already has the answer — `prerenderRoutes` returns `written`, one relative path per emitted page — so the shape of the fix is to merge those in. **What stops it being a two-line change is the FILTER**: `indexed` also drops `status: draft` and `robots: noindex`, and those are declared on the ROUTE, so a dynamic route marked noindex must not have its twelve pages appear just because they were emitted. That needs the emitted paths to carry their route id, which `written` does not. Related and not the same: a static build also emits the SPA entry bundle, which no prerendered page links — `dist/index.html` is overwritten by the prerendered home page — so a static target ships a client bundle nothing loads. Whether that should be emitted at all is the same question asked about a different artefact | [sitemap.js](packages/sierra/src/postbuild/sitemap.js) · [prerender.js](packages/sierra/src/build/prerender.js) · [FJS-455](#fjs-455) |
| <a id="fjs-422"></a>FJS-422 | sierra | **Every MULTI-WORD model was unpublishable on a `render: static` route, and the refusal said the schema does not describe a model the schema declares** | closed | 2026-08-23 | **Renumbered on filing** — a concurrent session issued the same number for an unrelated defect while this was being written. The static-safety gate taps what `load()` read and resolves each name through `modelNameFor`. Litestone's `$tapQuery` reports the TABLE, which is snake_case of the model name, and `registerSchemas` indexed four spellings — the model name, the accessor, `accessor + 's'` and the conventional plural — none of them the table. A one-word model resolved **by accident**, its table being its own accessor, which is why every fixture and every app in this repo passed: `product` → `Product`. `ProductVariant` is stored as `product_variant`, which resolved to `null`, and an unresolved read is `unknown`, and unknown **fails closed**. So `bun run build:public` in `example` refused the catalogue page with *read `product_variant`, which the schema does not describe, so its gate is unknown* — about a model on line 98 of `db/schema.lite` — and told the author to declare `publishes: 0`, which is the line that turns the check off. Failing closed is the right direction and is why this is not a leak; the cost is that the static target was unusable for any app with a two-word model, and that the message sent you to disable the gate. **Fixed 2026-08-23.** `registerSchemas` also indexes the snake_case table and its plural, deriving it the way `modelToTableName` does — restated in sierra rather than imported, because sierra may not depend on litestone (Invariant 1), and noted as not seeing `@@map`, which always wins on the Data side. The existing test asserted "resolves the table name the tap reports" using only one-word fixtures, so it passed throughout; the fixture now carries `ProductVariant` and `InvoiceLine`, and the second new case asserts that resolving the name does not turn the GATE off. Sierra would then get client-side transforms for free, which it also does not have today. | <a id="fjs-470"></a>FJS-470 | mesa | **Mesa carries three internal naming conventions with no principle between them, so `$foo` cannot be promised as an ordinary user name** | closed | 2026-08-24 | `FJS-D132` folds the twelve author-facing builtins onto one `$` object and deliberately leaves the compiler internals where they are, which means the rule it establishes is only two-thirds true. Three prefixes are in use and the split is historical rather than designed: **single `$`** holds the per-instance frame locals (`$runtime` 217 emit sites, `$dom` 82, `$option` 37, `$parentElement` 31, `$class` 29, `$domFirst` 18, `$domLast` 7 — 421 in all), **`$$`** holds roughly 150 uses across `$$sig_*`, `$$proxy_*`, `$$watch_*`, `$$set_*`, `$$snippet_*`, `$$item`, `$$v`, `$$e`, `$$arg0` plus the `$$eachBlock` / `$$virtualEach` / `$$delegate` runtime exports, and **`__`** holds 132 more in `__block`, `__anchor`, `__dev`, `__props`. A shape is discernible in the `$$` tier — it wears two dollars precisely where a name is generated from a user identifier or injected into a scope the author's own code runs in, which is the collision-avoidance job — but nothing enforces it and the single-`$` frame locals are in author scope too. **The fix is to converge on `$$`, not on `__`.** `__runtime` would say the internals are generic JavaScript noise; `$$runtime` says same family, one layer down, and it leaves the four-line rule true end to end: `$` is the door, `$:` / `$_name:` is the label, `$$` is the compiler's, everything else is yours. It also converts `FJS-D132`'s deferred win into a delivered one — `$foo` becomes an ordinary user variable name and `$$foo` becomes the reserved prefix nobody types by accident — and it makes RULE 31a (`$class` is compiler-internal, never declare it) enforceable rather than advisory. **Two things must be checked before a line moves.** Invariant 12 says compiler output is reproducible because scope ids are content-addressed, and CSS dedupe across the two compilers a static build runs depends on it — so whether emitted identifiers feed that hash decides whether every scope id in the repo moves at once. And `__` has to converge in the same pass or the three-tier story is fiction with a nicer name. **Checked and clear**: there is no `$$`-in-replacement-string trap, the only `.replace()` with a `$` in its replacement being a `'$1'` capture reference; and Chrome devtools' `$$` console helper is a distinct identifier that a `$$foo` local cannot shadow, so the single rule that follows is never to emit a bare `$$`. **Blast radius**: 421 sites in compiler emit strings and 15 test files that string-match `$runtime`; zero user `.mesa` files, since none of these names is author-facing. **Attempted 2026-08-24 and reverted — the rename is not a rename, which is the finding.** A blanket textual pass over the 430 emit sites went green on the compiler loading and then failed 84–257 tests in three waves, because this compiler encodes its own emitted identifiers in **four** ways and only one of them is a plain identifier. **Regex literals that match its own output**: `\$parentElement` is an ESCAPED dollar, so appending a second one produced `\$$parentElement`, where the new dollar reads as an end-of-input anchor mid-pattern — and the sibling case `\$\$sig_` is a legitimately doubled name that must NOT move, so the two cannot be told apart by a rule about dollars. **String keys paired with property reads**: `ctx.require('$emit')` is a key and `ctx.inuse.$emit` is a property, so a dot-guard that correctly protects the second silently renames only the first — and the failure is not an error but a binding that stops being emitted at all. **A cross-component protocol name**: `$class` is not a scope-local, it is the prop the `{class}` shorthand travels under, and `runtime.js` reads it as the string `'$class'` in `restProps`' skip set and as `$option.$class`; renaming it breaks class passthrough between two compiled components. **And the runtime's own exports** (`$onMount`, `$context`, `$tick` — seven of them) are the public API a composable helper imports and must keep the bare spelling, so they sit in the same file as names that should move. The tree was returned to green (mesa 1264 + 82 + 47, ui 857, sierra 1088, typecheck clean) and nothing of `FJS-D132` phases 0-5 was disturbed. **What this changes about the fix**: it is not a codemod. Each emitted name wants ONE definition the compiler reads — a constant, not a literal repeated across emit strings, regexes and registry keys — and that refactor is the actual work, with the rename falling out of it for free. Doing it the other way round is what was measured here. **Also cleared: Invariant 12 does not gate it.** `cssHash` is fed the style content and nothing else, deliberately, so renaming an emitted identifier cannot move a scope id. **Probed 2026-08-23, and the answer inverts the assumption**: the `$`-prefixed INTERNALS are clean — `$runtime`, `$dom`, `$class` and `$parentElement` compile and parse under `let`, `const`, `var` and `function` alike — while the injected BUILTINS collide, which is [FJS-471](#fjs-471) and is `FJS-D132`'s to fix. So this item carries no defect behind it and stays S4: it is a naming rule, not a bug. **Closed 2026-08-24, on the second attempt and after the ground moved.** What made it possible was doing the runtime's exports FIRST: `$runtime.$onMount` was two namespaces sharing a spelling, and once the export is `onMount` the local beside it can move without ambiguity. The rename then went through with the four encodings named above handled explicitly — plus a FIFTH the first attempt never reached, which is that `$$` inside a `.replace()` REPLACEMENT STRING collapses to one `$`, so `hoistTemplates` emitted `$tpl0 = $runtime.template(…)` from declarations that read `$$tpl0 = $$runtime.template(…)`; it is a callback now. Two more names turned out to be protocol rather than internal, in the sense `FJS-D134` had just ruled: `$dom` is the key a block factory answers with and `runtime.js` reads 59 times, and the three COMPILED door members (`$context`, `$inspect`, `$mounted`) are AUTHOR-space names after the pre-parse rewrite, so their injected locals have to keep the bare spelling to be reachable at all. Emitted output now carries no single-`$` name; `$runtime`, `$option` and `$dom` are ordinary user variable names, which is the sentence this issue existed to make true. `__anchor`/`__block`/`__props` did NOT converge and that half stays open below. Green: mesa 1265 + 82 + 47, ui 857, sierra 1088, jetty 46, cli 811, typecheck clean | [compiler.js:3076](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js#L3076) · [compiler.js:7503](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js#L7503) · [FJS-D132](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d132) |
| <a id="fjs-411"></a>FJS-411 | toolbelt · basecamp | **"2h ago" is written three times, and it is the same shape [FJS-408](#fjs-408) just closed — but it needs the `/datetime` argument first.** basecamp's cleanup, recipes and one other screen each carry a byte-identical `just now / 5m ago / 2h ago / 3d ago` ladder off `Date.now()`. They agree today, which is the state the byte formatter was in before it drifted. What stops it being the same one-hour fix is that a relative time is not a pure function of its argument: it reads the CLOCK, and toolbelt's whole standing is that every export is pure — same input, same output, no clock (`FJS-D26`). So the signature has to take `now` (honest, and every caller then passes `Date.now()`), or the kit stops being pure, or it lives somewhere else. Behind that sits the wider question the parked intent doc already frames: locale, timezone and whether `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` is the answer, in which case a hand-rolled English ladder is the wrong thing to standardise on | open | 2026-08-22 | [cleanup/index.mesa:131](packages/basecamp/web/src/routes/cleanup/index.mesa#L131) · [toolbelt/docs/datetime.md](packages/toolbelt/docs/datetime.md) · [FJS-D26](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d26) |
| <a id="fjs-390"></a>FJS-390 | ui · toolbelt | **An element that is busy has no owner, so it is hand-rolled 74 times.** Counted across the two apps in this tree (`packages/basecamp/web/src` + `example/web/src`): **76** declarations of a `busy` / `busyKey` / `saving` / `submitting` / `loading` / `pending` local, each paired with a `disabled={busy === row.id}` and a `finally` that has to put it back. **Recounted 2026-08-23** and the split is the part worth having: **69 of the 76 are basecamp's**, seven are `example`'s — `loading` 46, `busy` 26, `saving` 2, `pending` 1, `busyKey` 1. So this is not spread thin across two codebases, it is one app reaching for the same missing thing sixty-nine times, and the count rose while nobody was adding the feature. The affordance itself already exists — `<Button loading>` is a prop the kit ships — so what is missing is the thing that FLIPS it and restores it, and the restore is where the defect lives: an early return or a throw past the reset leaves a row disabled with nothing to re-enable it. **The repo has already chosen the shape once**: `toasts.loading(msg)` answers `{update, dismiss}` — a handle, not an id ([FJS-119](#fjs-119)) — because settling the thing you started is one value, not a lookup. `KOBAMI/my.maid.tech` arrived at the same answer for elements independently (`const release = Element.temporary(el)` → `release("There was an error")`), which is the useful kind of agreement. Open, and deliberately not designed here: whether it is an attachment in `@frontierjs/ui` (`{@attach busy}`), a pure function in toolbelt over a node, or a `<Form>`-shaped context the way `$context.form` already answers labels and errors. What is NOT open is the return type — it is a handle | open | 2026-08-22 | [Button.mesa:32](packages/ui/components/forms/Button.mesa#L32) · [FJS-119](#fjs-119) · [IDEAS/app-conventions-audit.md](IDEAS/app-conventions-audit.md) § 3.2 |
| <a id="fjs-376"></a>FJS-376 | basecamp | **Outpost replay protection is per PROCESS, so a second replica does not share it.** `requireOutpostSignature` verifies a signed machine request and rejects a nonce it has already seen; `seenNonces` is a module-level `Map`, swept on write. Two consequences, both stated in the code rather than discovered: two replicas each hold their own set, so a captured request replayed at the other one passes, and a restart forgets everything inside the freshness window. **Honest for what it currently defends** — the window is five minutes, the signature binds method, path, body hash and timestamp, and basecamp runs one process — which is why this is a row rather than a defect: it is a stated limit with a named trigger, and the trigger is *basecamp running more than one process*, which the deploy plane is heading towards. Closing it is a shared store, and the natural one already exists: the app's own SQLite database, keyed by nonce with the timestamp for the sweep, which is where the outbox and the job queue already put things that must survive a process. Sits beside the other half of the same seam — one secret for the whole fleet, so a compromised machine can forge any other machine's check-in ([FJS-257](#fjs-257) ring 1) | open | 2026-08-21 | [basecamp/api/src/core/hooks.ts:345](packages/basecamp/api/src/core/hooks.ts#L345) · [toolbelt/signature](packages/toolbelt/src/signature/signature.js) |
| <a id="fjs-373"></a>FJS-373 | cli · css | **Invariant 13 is the only styling rule the framework states and nothing checks it.** *Style with a tone and a treatment, never a colour* is enforced inside `packages/css` (a literal `font-size` outside `tokens.css` fails its suite) and nowhere in an app. Measured on `KOBAMI/my.maid.tech`, 56k lines written to this framework's ideas: it HAS the semantic layer — `theme-primary`, `theme-danger`, `.btn.outlined`, `.btn.danger`, arrived at independently — and uses **578 raw colour utilities against 156 semantic ones**, about 4:1 (`text-gray-500` 74, `bg-gray-100` 54, `text-red-600` 13). The vocabulary was not rejected; it was out-competed by whatever was quicker under deadline, which is what a rule with no check always loses to. `fli check` is the place — it already owns everything derived from the seed while Biome owns generic JavaScript (`FJS-D33`), and it reads `.mesa`, which Biome does not. Open question the rule has to answer before it can exist: an app may legitimately run UnoCSS alongside `@frontierjs/css` (`packages/css/README.md` § Using it with UnoCSS), so the check counts and reports a ratio rather than refusing a class — a warn naming the worst files, in the shape of `package-root-md`, which warns and names rather than failing because the rule cannot tell a stray from the next necessary thing | open | 2026-08-21 | [core/checks.js](packages/cli/core/checks.js) · [vocabulary.js](packages/css/vocabulary.js) · [IDEAS/app-conventions-audit.md](IDEAS/app-conventions-audit.md) § 2.3 |
