| <a id="fjs-d05"></a>FJS-D05 | litestone — **Multi-tenancy config API.** db-per-tenant only today; the config shape is undesigned | 2026-08-16 | **Ruled 2026-08-16, and built: tenancy is DECLARED in the seed — one `tenancy { }` block beside `database { }`, two strategies.** The filed question was a config shape; what it needed was an OWNER. db-per-tenant worked and its configuration lived in three places that could disagree — a `createTenantRegistry()` call, a `tenants:` slice in `litestone.config.js` the CLI read three keys of, and nothing in the schema — so `litestone tenant create` and the running app could each be right about a different directory. Precedence is now stated once: **option → declaration → default**. The other half was larger: **row-level tenancy was not a framework concept at all**, and basecamp hand-writes the rule on fifteen models. `strategy row` desugars into **`@@deny`, never `@@allow`** — allows are OR'd within an operation, so a generated allow would WIDEN every read to the whole tenant — plus a `@default(auth().<claim>)` stamp, and it is two rules because `checkCreatePolicy` runs before the stamp: an absent column is legitimate on create and belongs to nobody on read. `@@tenant(none)` / `@@tenant(column:)` answer what the block cannot judge, and models declaring neither are reported once by name. Resolution is asked rather than copied — `registry.tenantFor({host, headers, principal})` — and `createApp({ tenants })` installs `withTenantDb` in place of `withLitestoneDb`. Deferred with a reason: `check(parent)` delegation, [FJS-282](#fjs-282). `DECISIONS.md` § Access control — [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) § Access control · [docs/multi-tenancy.md](packages/litestone/docs/multi-tenancy.md) · [tenancy.js](packages/litestone/src/core/tenancy.js) |
| <a id="fjs-d06"></a>FJS-D06 | repo — **The coherence-review vocabulary proposals** | 2026-08-16 | **Ruled 2026-08-16.** Filed as one row, and it was never one question. **§1 `Hook` splits into three, not five** — Hook (may mutate or halt), Guard (allow/deny only), Observer (receives, cannot act) — on the axis the proposal named, which is the axis WordPress (`apply_filters`/`do_action`), Directus (filter/action hooks) and Payload (one access fn whose return type decides) all split on. None of them shipped more than two tiers and Rails/Prisma/Ember went the other way and removed mechanisms, so **Delegate is refused**: `onPasswordResetRequested` is the only one in the tree and auth's docs saying *required* costs nothing. The tier is the value; the renames (`conduit.hooks` → `observers`) are incidental. **§4 `Provider` is adopted with the plainer meaning — a third-party entity the app includes** (identity provider, payment provider), not the filed *swappable implementation of a contract*. The two nearly coincide here because almost every swappable implementation IS an integration point, and conduit already had the word for exactly this (`'provider'` target kind = external REST API). `Adapter` refused — it names a shape, not a party. **It must never mean Laravel's Service Provider**, which is this repo's **Plugin**. Two in-tree collisions cleared rather than tolerated: `app.provide` → **`app.claim`** (it never provided anything) and `_metricsProviders` → **`_metricsSources`** behind `app.registerMetricsSource`, which also retires §5's private-field reach-in. **§6**: Manifest ceded to MV3 — the Deployment half was already done (`Release`) and sierra's route table followed ([FJS-284](#fjs-284)); Gate and Policy both stay; **Channel is a broadcast set and a delivery medium is a Transport** ([FJS-285](#fjs-285)); `Edge` refused; `Trust Hierarchy` retired for *the gate ladder* ([FJS-286](#fjs-286)). **§7 `Slice` deferred** — Django Apps, Rails Engines and Phoenix Contexts each earned the name because a THIRD PARTY shipped one, and here both slices are in-repo. The map column is fixed regardless. §2/§3/§5/§8 keep their own ids. Unblocks [FJS-D10](#fjs-d10) — [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) § Naming & vocabulary · [IDEAS/coherence-review.md](IDEAS/coherence-review.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d09"></a>FJS-D09 | litestone — **Migrations, second tier** — rollback stance (`--backup`?), row-count assertion after rebuilds, second-granular timestamp collisions | 2026-08-16 | **Ruled 2026-08-16, and built.** **No `down`, ever**: a rebuild is a `DROP TABLE`, so the inverse of *drop a column* is *invent the values it held*, and a generated down that runs, succeeds and leaves a database only looking restored is worse than none. The way back is a copy taken first — `migrate apply --backup[=dir]` copies EVERY declared SQLite database before the FIRST is migrated (one taken as each database's turn comes round is a copy of a half-migrated fleet) and refuses to migrate anything if a copy fails; off by default, and without it apply NAMES what it cannot take back — a pending file that drops a table, and every `.js` migration, whose contents nothing can read. Whether the previous RELEASE can still serve is the other question and stays `release --from`'s. **Row count**: a copy reading fewer rows than the original holds is an error to nobody — SQLite inserted what it was asked, the runner saw a statement return — and `DROP TABLE` is the next line; the rebuild now compares the counts in between, as a CHECK on a one-row temp table whose constraint NAME is the message SQLite prints, since `RAISE()` is trigger-body only. **Timestamps**: filename order IS apply order, so a new migration is named after the last file in the directory rather than after the clock — same second, same label used to overwrite, same second, different labels applied alphabetically (`evolve` before `initial`). `DECISIONS.md` § Migrations — [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) § Migrations · [backup.js](packages/litestone/src/core/backup.js) · [migrate.js](packages/litestone/src/core/migrate.js) `rowCountGuard` · [migrations.js](packages/litestone/src/core/migrations.js) `nextMigrationName` |
| <a id="fjs-d10"></a>FJS-D10 | junction — **The deferred API cluster** | 2026-08-16 | **Ruled 2026-08-16 — two adopted, two refused, and the fourth was a defect wearing a naming question.** **`IAuth` partial acceptance — ADOPTED**: the interface declares six required methods and Junction invokes two (`verifySession` on both inbound paths, `sessionFor` behind `runAs`); the other four belong to `@frontierjs/auth`'s `/auth/*` routes and are called nowhere in the package, so an app was stubbing four methods that never run. `SessionVerifier = Pick<IAuth,'verifySession'> & Partial<IAuth>` is what `createApp({ auth })` and `setAuth()` take — DERIVED, because a hand-written member list drifts and because `Partial` is what keeps a fuller provider assignable past the excess-property check. **`publish()` string shorthand — REFUSED**: `channel: 'posts'` is already the declarative spelling and `svc.pipelines()` refuses a service declaring both, so a third spelling would arrive with a rule already policing the second. **Typed `createSchema` inference — REFUSED as filed, adopted cheap**: types come from the seed (a model service is validated by `autoValidate` off the JSON Schema and writes no schema at all), so `createSchema<T>()` takes the type the author already has and constrains the field map to its keys, rather than an inference engine competing with zod. **`createClient` options grouping — REFUSED** (it renames the container and leaves the precedence ladder exactly as subtle); **`setters`/`getters` → `read`/`write` adopted in name, and unblocked 2026-08-16** — grading the name found the mechanism reached 4 of its 16 declared operations, which is [FJS-288](#fjs-288); it reaches all seventeen now, so the rename is free to land — [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) § API design |
| <a id="fjs-d17"></a>FJS-D17 | sierra — **The UI plugin system is limited** — what else should a UI plugin be able to contribute? | 2026-08-16 | **Ruled 2026-08-16: a plugin contributes a CONTROL, and a control is two registrations in two packages.** `registerControl(name, resolve)` in sierra's `field-rules.js` says which control a column gets — a name, a descriptor, or null to decline; `registerFormControl(name, Component)` in `@frontierjs/ui/controls` says what that name renders as. The split is a dependency rule rather than a taste: field-rules is a leaf that has to run in plain Node (`formFields()` is called from a test, a prerender and a snapshot) and the kit peers only on mesa and css, so a NAME is the only thing that crosses. **The row's premise had also expired twice** — the field LIST shipped 2026-08-15, so it was not `make()` only; and the real limit was never the table's size but that it was a `switch` in a published package with an `{#if}` ladder behind it in a second one, so contributing a control meant forking both. The kit's five are now entries in the same table a contribution enters (`FormField.mesa`), and a registered name REPLACES a built-in of that name. Refused: a control over a `readOnly` column (the Data boundary refuses the write, so the form could not submit), and a resolver answering a component rather than a name. **The other two surfaces are the same mechanism and neither ships until its generator does** — a cell/detail renderer and a filter control, tracked at `IDEAS/overview.md` 1.1, because a registry with no consumer is a name nobody can call — [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) § UI substrate · [field-rules.js](packages/sierra/src/junction/field-rules.js) · [controls.js](packages/ui/controls.js) |
| <a id="fjs-129"></a>FJS-129 | ui — **`CommandPalette` declared its own `--cp-*` token namespace** | 2026-08-16 | 276 lines, 21 custom properties, five defined without reading a token — `--cp-radius: 12px`, `--cp-font: 'SF Mono', …`, `--cp-shadow: 0 24px 80px rgba(0,0,0,0.72)` — plus five literal colours and a paddings-and-font-sizes-in-px layout no theme or `.dense` could reach. Now **254 lines and two knobs** (`--cp-accent`, `--cp-font`), each reading a token: `--card-radius`, `--font-mono`, the `--text-*` ladder, the `--space-*` ladder, `--shadow-lg`, `--rule`, `--surface-raised`, `--motion-fast`. **Three of its own things turned out to be shipped ones.** The keycaps were a local `.cp-kbd` while `code.css` styles `<kbd>` on the ELEMENT, so deleting the class was the whole fix; the badge is `.badge` with a tone; and the active row's three hand-mixed `color-mix()` calls are the package's TINT ramp — set `--bg-mix` and `--tint-surface`/`--tint-rule`/`--tint-ink` follow, already contrast-measured across every theme. **The scrim became a token in `@frontierjs/css`** (`--scrim`, tokens.css): the palette dimmed at 0.72 while every `<dialog>` in the package dimmed at 0.45, and neither could be retuned by a theme. It is a literal rather than a blend of `--ink` because a scrim never inverts — mixed from ink it goes white on a dark theme. Classes are `fjs-cp-*` now, and **two drives named the old ones** — `example`'s and `basecamp`'s — which is the cost of renaming a kit class and is worth knowing before the next rename: a component's class names are part of its published surface the moment anything drives it. `basecamp`'s went unnoticed until its own drive ran, because grepping the apps' `src/` finds nothing; the selectors live in `web/test/`. 27 assertions in the kit drive, seven of them the failures above asked as measurements | 
| <a id="fjs-305"></a>FJS-305 | ui — **`CommandPalette` handled every keypress twice** | 2026-08-16 | `on:keydown={handleKeydown}` was bound on the backdrop AND on the search input, and keydown bubbles — so one press ran the handler twice. Every arrow key **skipped a row**, and **Enter ran the chosen command twice**, which for a command palette is the difference between one order and two. Invariant 11 says the nearest delegation root owns an event and a handler must fire once; this is that rule inside a single component. Found by the first spec to press ArrowDown twice and check WHICH row was active — `example`'s `verify:ui` has driven this palette since it was written and asserts that it opens, filters and runs a command, none of which can see a doubled press. The handler now lives on the backdrop only, where it still sees the input's keys by bubbling, and the drive counts invocations rather than trusting an idempotent action |
| <a id="fjs-128"></a>FJS-128 | ui — **`DatePicker` was a second design system inside the first** | 2026-08-16 | 468 lines of local `<style>` declaring **107 custom properties, 47 of them defined without reading a single design token** — its own six-rung radius scale, its own font-size scale, `font-family: sans-serif`, eight literal colours, and four **base64 PNG** nav arrows, which are black pixels and therefore invisible on a dark theme and unable to follow a tone. Now **320 lines and six declared properties**, four of which are the documented knobs (`--dp-cell`, `--dp-accent`, `--dp-band`, `--dp-layer`) and each reads a token. Zero literal colours, zero base64. Radii come from `--pill-radius`/`--card-radius`/`--field-radius`, type from `--font-primary` and the `--text-*` ladder, spacing from `--space-*` **so `.dense` reaches the whole panel**, elevation from `--shadow-lg`, and the focus ring from `--ring-*` — drawn here rather than restated as a recipe, because `focus.css` opts elements in BY CLASS and cannot name a class this kit invented. **The selected day is `.chip`**, so `--fill`/`--on-fill` derive its text colour against any tone a theme defines instead of assuming white. Every internal class is now `fjs-dp-*`, so `.disabled`, `.start` and `.end` are only ever compounds of one — the file no longer writes a selector that reads like package vocabulary. **The blocker was the safety net and it was built first**: `FJS-028`'s drive covers this component with 30 assertions, six of them the failures above asked as measurements — a theme switch moves the painted colours, `.dense` moves the padding, overriding `--dp-cell` moves the disc, the grid track AND the band's bleed, and the arrows are SVG rather than a background image. | <a id="fjs-297"></a>FJS-297 | mesa — **`close`, `cancel`, `toggle`, `beforetoggle` and `invalid` were DELEGATED, so `<dialog on:close>` never fired** | 2026-08-16 | Found the hour `@frontierjs/ui` got a browser drive of its own ([FJS-028](#fjs-028)), by the first spec that closed a `Drawer`. | <a id="fjs-300"></a>FJS-300 | ui — **`DatePicker` threw on month navigation whenever the new month had fewer weeks** | 2026-08-16 | Both panes iterated `{#each { length: 6 }}` and read `calendar[week][day]` from inside an `{#if calendar[week]}` guard. Fixed by iterating the weeks the month actually has (`{#each startDateCalendar as week}`), which also deletes the fixed-six assumption; the general ordering hazard is [FJS-303](#fjs-303). Headings gain `--heading-font-weight`, written as the fallback arm exactly like `--heading-letter-spacing`, so unset keeps h1/h2 at 700 and the rest at 600 and one declaration moves all six. That closes the row's own framing — the SIZE ladder was themable and the treatment was not |
| <a id="fjs-162"></a>FJS-162 | css — **motion is not themable — no duration and no easing token** | 2026-08-16 | Four rungs named for the job rather than the length — `--motion-fast` (a colour, a border, a background changing), `--motion-base` (a control changing shape), `--motion-enter` (something arriving or leaving), `--motion-slow` (a measurement moving) — plus `--motion-spin`, `--motion-shimmer` and three easings. **A spinner's easing is deliberately not a token**: `linear` at the use site, because a spinner that eases reads as broken hardware rather than a slower spinner. The row is right that `prefers-reduced-motion` is a different question, and the test says so — a literal duration INSIDE that query is correct (it is the reader's setting, and `tokens.css` hands the spinner back a slower 1.6s there), so the sweep for literals excludes it by walking the parent rules, and excludes the harness's own `transition: none !important` as the one stylesheet on the page with no `href`. That sweep found one the grep did not: `.skeleton`'s 1.4s shimmer. **The overlay tier caught the alias trap in the act** — the first version aliased `--overlay-time: var(--motion-enter)` at `:root`, which resolves once and inherits past every `.theme-*`, so a theme retuning the ladder would have moved everything except the overlays; it is read at each use site instead, which puts a comma inside a transition segment and turned `overlays.spec.js` red against CSS that was exactly right |
| <a id="fjs-094"></a>FJS-094 | caravan — **a job file could not declare its schedule** | 2026-08-16 | `HandlerOptions` grows `cron` and `timeZone`, so a registration says WHEN it runs beside WHAT it does, and `defineJob` passes both through — a `*.job.ts` file now declares everything about itself and needs no line in `app.ts`. The schedule is registered in **`handle()`** rather than in `schedule()`, which is the part that makes it reachable at all: `handle` is the only call autoload makes. `schedule(name, expr, fn)` is now sugar over it, so a cron declared in a file and one declared in a call are the same registration rather than two paths that can disagree. A name is a schedule and not a list of them — re-registering replaces, where two `schedule()` calls with one name used to fire the job twice a minute — and an unparseable expression is refused naming the JOB, because the expression alone no longer says which file to open. `example/api/jobs/sweep-abandoned.ts` was named `.ts` to keep it out of the autoload glob; it is `sweep-abandoned.job.ts` now, the `queue.schedule(…)` line in `example/api/app.ts` is gone, and `verify:jobs` passes 8/8 with the cron read off the file — [caravan/src/index.ts](packages/caravan/src/index.ts) · [tests/declaration.test.ts](packages/caravan/tests/declaration.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-093"></a>FJS-093 | caravan — **a job handler could not reach the app, and ran as nobody** | 2026-08-16 | `defineJob('x', async (job) => …)` was called with one argument — no `job.app`, no second parameter, and `autoload.ts` imported the file without passing anything — so an autoloaded `*.job.ts` could not call `app.service(…)`. In this framework that is what a job mostly wants: the service layer is the one place a write is announced ([FJS-D21](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-d21)), and a job reaching for `db.asSystem()` instead writes silently ([FJS-010](#)). Caravan is handed `app` in its own `register(app)` and kept it; `example/api/app-ref.ts` was the workaround — a module-level back-reference `app.ts` set and job files read, saying so in its own header. **A handler now takes a `JobContext`**: the job's facts plus `app` and `auth`. `ctx.app` closes the filed half; `ctx.auth` closes the one filed beside it in `caravan/CLAUDE.md` — a job had no principal, and no principal is STRANGER(0), so every job in every app carried `{ auth: { user: SYSTEM } }` by hand, which *also* ran a customer's work with the authority of the shop. `dispatch()` records `app.principal()?.userId` in an `actor_id` column and the worker runs the handler inside `app.runAs(actorId, …)`, so a service call in a handler names no `auth` and inherits one. **An id, not a session** — a caller demoted between asking and running is graded at what they hold now, where a snapshot is a privilege outliving its own revocation. Nobody asked (cron, boot) resolves to `createApp({ system })`; an unresolvable actor fails the job by name rather than downgrading. Proved in `example`'s audit trail: the `book-courier` write names the staff member who pressed Ship, where every background write used to say `system`. `example/api/app-ref.ts` is deleted — [caravan/CHANGES.md](packages/caravan/CHANGES.md) 2026-08-16 · [tests/job-context.test.ts](packages/caravan/tests/job-context.test.ts) · [junction/CHANGES.md](packages/junction/CHANGES.md) |
| <a id="fjs-090"></a>FJS-090 | caravan — **a job's name was stated up to three times and nothing reconciled them** | 2026-08-16 | Two halves. **The file is the reconciliation**: autoload compares the `defineJob` name against the file it is in and THROWS on a mismatch, naming both and saying which to rename — `send-emial.job.ts` used to register a handler no dispatch ever reached, and the only symptom was a job that silently never ran. **The definition is the dispatch handle**: `defineJob` returns a `JobDefinition<T>` and `dispatch(bookCourier, { orderId })` takes the name off it, so a call site states no string and `data` is typed by the handler that will receive it rather than being `unknown` on both sides. `handle(definition)` registers one whole, options and cron included. Dispatch-by-name still works — a separate worker process holding the handler is a real shape and refusing it would be worse. `example`'s two dispatch sites now import the definitions, which also deletes the hand-written `ctx.app as { jobs?: … }` cast at each: importing a job file puts Caravan's augmentation of `app.jobs` in the program — [caravan/src/autoload.ts](packages/caravan/src/autoload.ts) · [example/api/services/orders.service.ts](example/api/services/orders.service.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-048"></a>FJS-048 | caravan — **the `junction.config.js` caravan section was partial** | 2026-08-16 | `register()` honours every key of it now: `db`, `jobsDir`, `cleanupAfter`, `pollInterval`, `queues`, `admin`. What blocked four of them was that `createCaravan()` opened the database and built the workers at construction, before any app existed to hand a config over. **The database opens on first use** and **the workers are built in `start()`**, after autoload — so nothing a config file sets has been spent by the time `register()` reads it, and a queue named by a config file, a job file or a runtime dispatch all arrive the same way. Two adjacent defects came out with it: the merge tested truthiness, so `cleanupAfter: 0` — the way to turn the sweep off — read as unset from either side (now `=== undefined`, and `admin: false` with it); and `stop()` closed the database while the pool kept the workers holding it, so a `start()` after a `stop()` polled through a closed handle. A dispatch before `configure()` opens the queue early and is warned about by path rather than silently running against the wrong file. 8 tests — [caravan/src/index.ts](packages/caravan/src/index.ts) · [tests/junction-integration.test.ts](packages/caravan/tests/junction-integration.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-256"></a>FJS-256 | cli — **`fli ws:atlas` links `@frontierjs/css` from unpkg, so the page does not render without the network** | 2026-08-16 | Inlined, built from committed source on every run — `src/index.css` is 48 `@import … layer(name)` lines plus the `@layer a, b, c;` declaration, so each import becomes an `@layer name { … }` block and the declaration is carried over first and verbatim (without it, layer order falls back to first appearance, which agrees with the declaration only by accident of import order — `packages/css/build.js` refuses to ship a bundle missing it for the same reason). **Not `dist/`, which the row proposed and which is that file already**: it is gitignored and built on demand, so inlining-if-present and linking-if-absent makes the OUTPUT depend on whether somebody ran `bun run build` — and these pages are committed snapshots the `snapshots` phase regenerates and diffs, so two machines would disagree about a file neither changed. Measured while fixing it: the tree's `dist/` held a two-day-old `frontier.css` and **no `frontier.min.css` at all**, which is the same defect wearing a stale copy. **Every import must resolve or the bundle is refused**, stated as *all of them* rather than as a floor — a count is a guess a small tree fails and a truncated big one passes — because a partial bundle renders, looks nearly right, and is missing whichever layer went absent. Comments are stripped by a quote-aware pass (60% of that package's source; `content: "/*"` is legal CSS and is asserted). **The row's third edge closed first and painfully**: the derived range was not merely interim, it was wrong — below 1.0 a caret pins the MINOR, so `@^0.16` means `>=0.16.0 <0.17.0` and excluded every published copy the day css bumped its minor in the tree. Measured that day: `@^0.16` 404, no range 302. The link survives only as the fallback for a tree that cannot read the package at all, and carries no range. Verified by loading the generated page in headless Chrome with no stylesheet request in it: `body` at the field theme's own ground, `--surface` resolving, `.btn` taking its padding off the space ladder. 4 new tests; the two that sliced the local CSS at the first `<style>` now name it by id, one of which had started passing vacuously — [repo-map.js](packages/cli/core/repo-map.js) · [repo-atlas.js](packages/cli/core/repo-atlas.js) |
| <a id="fjs-067"></a>FJS-067 | mesa — **the deferred set: `tick()`, `{#virtual each}` in SSR, variable-height virtual lists, full hydration SSR, TypeScript** | 2026-08-16 | Four of the five were never open, and the fifth was not what the row said. **`$tick()` ships** and is tested (`runtime.js`, `runtime.test.js` §4) — it chains onto a resolved promise, which lands the callback after the flush microtask a signal write already queued. **Full hydration SSR and TypeScript are deferred by the language spec** (VISION RULE 20), and **variable-height rows by RULE 34** — those are roadmap, which this register says belongs in `IDEAS/`, not defects. **`{#virtual each}` produces no SSR output is simply false**: `renderToHTML` sets `isBrowser` true so the block runs, and a 1000-row list measured 25 rows plus a 39000px bottom spacer — a prerendered page gets its first screen, which is what the row wanted a `{:static}` fallback to provide. Five documents and two source comments said *renders nothing*; all seven now say what it does. `$: fn()` post-execution hooks was an orphan phrase with no referent anywhere in the repo. **What the audit did find is a real defect and is fixed**: the documented options (`height=N viewport="500px"`, VISION §9.7, required by RULE 34) were swallowed into the `as` binding, so every component written from the docs died at compile time as `Unexpected identifier 'height'` — naming neither the block, nor the option, nor the file's real problem. They parse now, at bracket depth 0 so a destructuring default is not mistaken for one; an unknown option and a non-positive height are refused by name; `height` skips measurement (a row sized by a stylesheet measures 0 on first paint) and `viewport` sizes the block's own element, which IS the scroller. 8 tests, both halves mutation-checked |
| <a id="fjs-291"></a>FJS-291 | mesa — **the Vite plugin's virtual CSS module could not fire, and `css: false` dropped the stylesheet on the floor** | 2026-08-16 | The route is deleted rather than repaired. The compiler's `css` is a **destination, not a switch** — truthy inlines the scoped rules as `$runtime.addStyles(id, …)`, falsy extracts them onto `ctx.css.result` — and the plugin was passing its own option straight through while reading the answer as if the word meant the same thing on both sides, so `if (css && ctx.css?.result)` was a condition no compiler could satisfy. Gone with it: `cssCache`, the `?mesa-css` id, its `resolveId`/`load` pair and `handleHotUpdate`'s CSS invalidation. **Inlining is the one route, and it is the one Sierra's plugin takes for the same file** — the ids are content-addressed, which is what lets a prerendered page and the client agree about which styles are already on the page (Invariant 12); a second delivery route here would have made one `.mesa` file's CSS arrive differently depending on which plugin compiled it. `css: false` now means what it says — the block is compiled and DROPPED — and it is stated in the option doc, the README and a test, instead of being a silent consequence of a branch nobody could reach. Its one live victim was `mesa-bench`, whose config set `css: false` to avoid virtual CSS modules and thereby rendered every bench component unstyled; the option is off its config now. Pinned in three places: the inline path over a real dev server (`test/vite-server.test.js`), the absent second route and the honest `false` (`test/vite-plugin.test.js`), and an extracted stylesheet having no reader (`test/vite-compiler-resolution.test.js`). Wiring Vite-owned stylesheets up remains possible and is filed rather than dropped: `IDEAS/css-delivery.md` (overview 5.20) holds what it would buy, why it cannot be done in one plugin alone, and the measurement it wants first |

## See also

- `ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md` — closed rows aged out of § Closed. Same table, verbatim. Read when tracing a specific id, never cold.
- `CLAUDE.md` § Live hazards — traps that are **correct behaviour**; knowing them saves a day. Not issues.
- `DECISIONS.md` — settled rulings. A `FJS-D##` closing here lands there.
- `IDEAS/` — roadmap and design records for work not started. `IDEAS/overview.md`'s wave 0 is the subset of this file that happens to be argued in an ideas file.
- `HANDOFF.md` — per-session narrative, newest first. History, not a ledger.
- `VERIFYING.md` — how to promote a `stale?` row to `open` or `closed`.
